<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove rational and $`\overline{\mathbb Q}_\ell`$ decompositions of cuspidal automorphic functions for split semisimple groups over global function fields into subspaces indexed by nilpotent orbits of the dual group. The decompositions hold at every full finite level, with arbitrary divisor multiplicities. Each summand is governed by the same orbit at every unramified place and every complex embedding. At trivial level this proves Conjectures 3.4.5 and 3.4.6 of Gaitsgory–Lafforgue–Raskin. For split connected adjoint absolutely simple groups, we also prove that a cuspidal automorphic representation with one generic unramified local component is tempered at every unramified place. Thus globally generic cuspidal representations in this scope satisfy the unramified part of the generalized Ramanujan conjecture.

- **claim-main-decomposition** (theorem, [source](#stmt-main-decomposition)): For every $X,G,D,\ell$ as above, the natural addition maps $$\label{intro:decomposition} \bigoplus_{\mathcal O}C_{D,\mathcal O,\mathbb Q} \xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }C_{D,\mathbb Q}, \qquad \bigoplus_{\mathcal O}C_{D,\mathcal O,\ell} \xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }C_{D,\ell}$$ are isomorphisms. The sums range over the nilpotent orbits of $\widehat G$.
  - Explanation: The cuspidal function space at any full finite level splits as a direct sum of spectral-support subspaces indexed by nilpotent orbits of the dual group, with rational and l-adic coefficients.
- **claim-main-generic-ramanujan** (theorem, [source](#stmt-main-generic-ramanujan)): Let $F$ be the function field of a smooth projective geometrically connected curve over a finite field, and let $G/F$ be split connected adjoint absolutely simple. Let $\pi=\bigotimes'_x\pi_x$ be a complex cuspidal automorphic representation of $G(\mathbb A_F)$. If $\pi_v$ is unramified and generic at one place $v$, then $\pi_x$ is tempered at every place $x$ at which it is unramified. In particular, every globally generic cuspidal automorphic representation of $G(\mathbb A_F)$ is tempered at every unramified place.
  - Explanation: A cuspidal automorphic representation of a split adjoint absolutely simple group that is unramified and generic at one place is tempered at every place where it is unramified.
- **claim-cat-specialization** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cat-specialization)): For every prime $p\ne\ell$, the geometric characteristic-zero and $\overline{\mathbf F}_p$ categories $\mathscr H_{P,Q}$ admit simultaneous enhanced equivalences preserving standard, costandard, and intersection complexes, convolution, and change of parahoric level. They also preserve restriction of equivariance from $L^+G$ to the pro-unipotent radical $I^u$ of an Iwahori, including the induced maps of mapping complexes. These are assertions about ordinary geometric categories; Weil structures will be specified separately.
  - Explanation: Enhanced categories of constructible complexes on parahoric double quotients agree between characteristic zero and positive characteristic, compatibly with standard objects, convolution and change of level.
- **claim-cat-satake** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cat-satake)): The stable idempotent-complete tensor category generated by the normalized spherical Satake complexes is monoidally equivalent to $$\operatorname{Perf}^{\widehat G}(S).$$ The normalized Satake object $\operatorname{Sat}(V)$ corresponds to $S\otimes V$. With the split Weil structures, Frobenius pullback corresponds to extension of scalars by $$S\longrightarrow S,\qquad \alpha\longmapsto q\alpha \quad(\alpha\in\widehat{\mathfrak g}^*[-2]),$$ and acts trivially on the representation labels. These assertions include the enhanced monoidal structure and all morphisms between the indicated objects.
  - Explanation: Derived Satake: spherical Satake complexes form a tensor category equivalent to equivariant perfect modules over the shifted symmetric algebra, with Frobenius acting by dilation.
- **claim-cat-central** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cat-central)): There is a tensor functor to the Drinfeld center of the homotopy category $$Z:\operatorname{Rep}(\widehat G)\longrightarrow \mathcal Z(\operatorname{Ho}(\mathscr H_{I,I}))$$ with its split Weil structure. Its interchanges are compatible with projection to every standard parahoric. At a hyperspecial parahoric the projected functor is Satake.* *There are invertible kernels $J_a$, $a\in\Lambda$, with coherent multiplication $J_a\star J_b\simeq J_{a+b}$. They are standard on the positive chamber and costandard on its opposite. The central object $Z(V)$ has a finite functorial Wakimoto filtration, tensor compatible in $V$, with $$\operatorname{gr}_a Z(V)=J_a\otimes V_a.$$ Its induced central interchange with $J_b$ is the ordinary interchange of the two translation factors and the identity on the multiplicity space, with the fixed Satake parity convention. The multiplicity spaces have trivial semisimplified Frobenius. These statements hold over every $\mathbf F_q$ under consideration. For a fixed central label, its interchange in the other kernel variable is a chosen natural equivalence of exact enhanced functors. We use the central tensor identities in the homotopy category; we do not assert an enhancement of $Z$ to an infinity-categorical Drinfeld center.
  - Explanation: Representations of the dual group give central objects in the Drinfeld center of the homotopy category, with a Wakimoto filtration whose graded pieces are translation kernels tensored with weight spaces.
- **claim-cat-springer** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cat-springer)): Let $\operatorname{For}$ forget $L^+G$-equivariance to $I^u$-equivariance on $\operatorname{Gr}_G$, without changing the underlying complex, and let $\operatorname{Av}_*$ be its right adjoint. Put $W_\eta=J_\eta\star\delta_0$. For every integral weight $\eta$ in the closed costandard chamber, derived Satake identifies $$\operatorname{Av}_*W_\eta \quad\text{with}\quad \bigl(R\pi_*\mathcal O_{\widetilde{\mathcal N}}(\eta)\bigr)_{\rm sh}, \qquad \pi:\widetilde{\mathcal N}\longrightarrow\widehat{\mathfrak g}.$$ The subscript means that coherent degree $a$ and polynomial degree $b$ are assigned total degree $a+2b$. This is an equivalence of ordinary equivariant dg $S$-modules. The $S$-action is polynomial restriction along $\pi$, and the assertion is compatible with tensoring by representations and with all morphisms between free Satake labels. The displayed convention introduces no extra shift. Any smooth-pullback normalization of $\operatorname{For}$ changes the formula only by a fixed shift and Tate twist, independent of $\eta$.
  - Explanation: Under derived Satake, averaged Wakimoto objects for weights in the costandard chamber correspond to sheared pushforwards of line bundles on the Springer resolution.
- **claim-cat-cyclic** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cat-cyclic)): The preceding construction is an exact enhanced Frobenius-twisted cyclic functor. Put $\Phi=\mathrm{Fr}^*$ on local kernels. With convolution ordered in the direction of the path, its cyclic maps are $$\label{cat:last-first} \rho_{U,V}:\mathsf K(U\star V) \xrightarrow{\sim}\mathsf K(\Phi(V)\star U).$$ They are natural in all enhanced morphisms and coherently compatible with units, iterated products, and change of level. Here $\rho_{U,\mathbf1}$ is the identity. The full slide $\rho_{\mathbf1,V}:\mathsf K(V)\to\mathsf K(\Phi V)$, followed by the Weil structure of $V$, is cohomological Frobenius. Slides at disjoint sets of legs commute; their product is the full slide.
  - Explanation: The compact cohomology of paths from a bundle to its Frobenius transform is a coherent cyclic functor, with rotation maps whose full slide is cohomological Frobenius.
- **claim-cat-hecke** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cat-hecke)): Suppose that $L$ has a central label at a closed place $x$. The operators in Equation <a href="#cat:character" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="cat:character">[cat:character]</a> give the usual affine Hecke algebra at $x$, with parameter $q_x$, on $H^\bullet\mathsf K(L)$. They give ordinary Hecke operators on $H^\bullet\mathsf K(\mathbf1)$.* *More explicitly, let $H_w$ denote the operator with characteristic function $1_{IwI}$ and Iwahori volume one. Then $$H_s^2=(q_x-1)H_s+q_x, \qquad H_wH_{w'}=H_{ww'}\quad \text{if }\ell(ww')=\ell(w)+\ell(w').$$ The positive normalized Bernstein translations are $$\label{cat:translation-sign} T_a=(-1)^{\ell(t^a)}\chi_{J_a}, \qquad T_a=q_x^{-\ell(t^a)/2}H_{t^a}\quad(a\text{ positive}),$$ and extend multiplicatively to the entire translation lattice.* *For a standard parahoric $P\supset I$, its two level transfers identify the hyperspecial or parahoric trace cohomology with the image of $$e_P=\left(\sum_{w\in W_P}q_x^{\ell(w)}\right)^{-1} \sum_{w\in W_P}H_w.$$ At hyperspecial level the spherical character action agrees with the Bernstein center. The individual-leg slides commute with these actions and transfers. On the Wakimoto graded terms the Bernstein action is the translation action tensored with the weight multiplicities in Theorem <a href="#cat:central" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cat:central">5</a>. The Wakimoto filtration gives long exact sequences equivariant for these Bernstein translations and for the selected-leg slides. Its graded terms carry the stated translation action. Thus flat completion, ordered-weight projections, and spectral projectors may be applied to these cohomology sequences.* *There is also the following geometric form of the rank-one assertion. If an exact translation position is parallel to the simple wall of $s$, the operator $H_s+1$ on its path trace is pullback and sum along the finite etale family of Frobenius-fixed rank-one refinement flags, of degree $q_x+1$. Consequently $H_s$ takes the sum over the other flags.
  - Explanation: Character operators from rigid kernels realize the affine Hecke algebra on the cohomology of the trace, with Bernstein translations, parahoric transfers and a rank-one flag description.
- **claim-cat-loops** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cat-loops)): At a hyperspecial closed-place leg of degree $d$, the functor $\mathsf K$ on perfect spherical labels has the form $$\label{cat:loop-pairing} \mathsf K(P)\simeq \left(\mathcal A\otimes_{B_{q^d}} (B_{q^d}\otimes_{S^{\otimes d}}P)\right)^{\widehat G},$$ where $\mathcal A$ is an equivariant dg module, with no coherence or boundedness assumption. After eliminating intermediate factors, the algebra is $$\label{cat:loop-algebra} B_r=\mathcal O(\widehat G)\otimes \operatorname{Sym}(\widehat{\mathfrak g}^*[-2]\oplus\widehat{\mathfrak g}^*[-1]), \qquad r=q^d.$$ Write $s$ for its degree-zero holonomy variable and $e$ for its degree-two linear variable. The second summand supplies odd Koszul generators $\eta_\alpha$ of degree $1$, with $$\label{cat:holonomy} d\eta_\alpha= \langle\alpha,\operatorname{Ad}(s)e-q^d e\rangle.$$ The group acts by simultaneous conjugation.* *The $d$-fold selected-leg slide on representation labels is the action of $s$ on each factor. Spherical Hecke characters act by the corresponding invariant characters of $s$. On cohomology these descriptions agree with the transfers in Proposition <a href="#cat:hecke" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cat:hecke">8</a>. A degree-two morphism linear in the selected $e$ variable multiplies the corresponding slide eigenvalue by $q^d$. All statements are natural in perfect labels and their dg morphisms.
  - Explanation: At a hyperspecial leg the Frobenius trace on spherical labels is a pairing with an equivariant dg module over a dg loop algebra whose Koszul differential encodes the resonance relation.
- **claim-amp-geometry** (theorem, [source](#stmt-amp-geometry)): For a fixed split connected reductive group and fixed finite level, the following statements hold.* 1. *The number of rational isomorphism classes of bundles of instability at most $b$ is bounded by $C(1+b)^N$, for fixed $C,N$.* 2. *There is a locally finite constructible stratification of the bundle stack such that, on a stratum where the automorphism group has dimension $r$, the stratum has dimension at most $C-r$. The same constant works for all Harder–Narasimhan types.* 3. *Suppose that a sequence of bundles $\mathcal E_n$ has meromorphic isomorphisms to its point-Frobenius transforms, or meromorphic automorphisms, with the $n$th modification of size at most $b_n$. If the ratio of the instability of $\mathcal E_n$ to $1+b_n$ is unbounded, a subsequence has a coarsening of the canonical reduction to one fixed maximal parabolic that these isomorphisms carry to its point-Frobenius transform, or to itself, respectively. The assertion holds over geometric points and is compatible with descent and iteration of the Frobenius isomorphism.* *All statements allow an arbitrary effective level divisor, including nonreduced divisors, and any fixed list of parahoric flags.
  - Explanation: Bounded geometry of bundle stacks: polynomial count of rational classes by instability, dimension control of strata, and a preserved maximal parabolic reduction under large instability.
- **claim-amp-trace-bound** (theorem, [source](#stmt-amp-trace-bound)): Let $\mathcal B$ be one of the bundle stacks in Proposition <a href="#amp:geometry" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="amp:geometry">10</a>. The object $\Delta_!\mathbf k$ on $\mathcal B\times\mathcal B$ has a uniform upper perverse bound. Moreover, for a constructible complex $\mathcal F$ on $\mathcal B\times\mathcal B$ with upper perverse bound $N$, pullback to the point-Frobenius graph followed by compact cohomology has upper bound $N+C_1$, where $C_1$ is independent of $\mathcal F$.
  - Explanation: The diagonal pushforward has a uniform upper perverse bound, and pulling back to the Frobenius graph with compact cohomology costs only a constant.
- **claim-amp-tests** (theorem, [source](#stmt-amp-tests)): In the hyperspecial cyclic calculation, a fixed list of perfect spherical labels has bounded-above cohomology. The same upper bound property holds uniformly when the list also contains a fixed number of Springer pushforwards $$R\pi_*\mathcal O_{\widetilde{\mathcal N}}(\eta_j), \qquad \pi:\widetilde{\mathcal N}\longrightarrow\widehat{\mathfrak g},$$ whose line bundles are independently nef. Fixed additional twists or fixed perfect factors are allowed. The number of factors and the additional twists are fixed; their nef weights may vary independently without changing the upper bound.
  - Explanation: Hyperspecial cyclic calculations against fixed spherical labels and nef Springer pushforwards have cohomology bounded above uniformly in the nef weights.
- **claim-amp-specialization** (theorem, [source](#stmt-amp-specialization)): After this specialization, the cyclic calculation is a pairing with an $H$-equivariant dg module $M$ over $\operatorname{Sym}(E_t^*[-2])$. The module need not be coherent or bounded. The action of a spherical polynomial at any one geometric factor is restriction of its linear functions to $E_t$. The uniform tests of Proposition <a href="#amp:tests" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="amp:tests">12</a> remain valid. They also allow fixed equivariant perfect factors on the flag varieties, in particular restriction to fixed closed $H^\circ$-orbits of Borels.
  - Explanation: After finite Koszul specialization at a semisimple class, the cyclic calculation becomes a pairing with an equivariant dg module over the symmetric algebra of the resonant space, keeping the uniform tests.
- **claim-amp-levi** (theorem, [source](#stmt-amp-levi)): For every such $L$ and every fixed finite-dimensional rational $H_L$-representation $U$, there is a constant $C(U,L)$ such that $$\label{amp:levi-bound} H^i\bigl((M_L\otimes U\otimes\eta)^{H_L}\bigr)=0 \quad\text{for }i>C(U,L),\qquad \eta\in X^*(L).$$ Invariants denote invariant global sections. The constant is independent of the character $\eta$.
  - Explanation: Invariant cohomology of the specialized module twisted by any character of the Levi is bounded above by a constant independent of the character.
- **claim-amp-orbits** (theorem, [source](#stmt-amp-orbits)): Every element of $E_L$ is nilpotent and admits such a compatible triple. There are finitely many $H_L$-orbits in $E_L$. For $e\in E_L$, every $h_e$-weight in the normal space $$N_e=E_L/[e,\operatorname{Lie}H_L]$$ is nonpositive.
  - Explanation: Resonant elements are nilpotent with compatible triples, there are finitely many orbits, and normal weights are nonpositive.
- **claim-amp-support** (theorem, [source](#stmt-amp-support)): Suppose $F_e^{\mathrm{corr}}$ has upper bound $B_e$. For every finite-dimensional $H_L$-representation $V$, the contribution of the orbit $H_Le$ to invariant sections of $M_L\otimes V$ has upper bound $$\label{amp:orbit-bound} B_e+C_e+\max\{\text{$h_e$-weights of }V\},$$ where $C_e$ is independent of $V$. This assertion uses local cohomology in an open subset where the orbit is closed and is valid for arbitrary dg $M_L$.
  - Explanation: Given a bound for the corrected fiber at one orbit, the orbit's contribution to invariant sections is bounded by that bound plus a constant plus the largest neutral weight of the test representation.
- **claim-amp-fiber** (theorem, [source](#stmt-amp-fiber)): For every allowed Levi $L$ and every $e\in E_L$, the corrected fiber $F_e^{\mathrm{corr}}$ is bounded above. The bound is a bound on the whole complex; its cohomology need not be finite-dimensional.
  - Explanation: Every corrected fiber is bounded above, proved by induction on semisimple rank using the open orbit and boundary orbits.
- **claim-amp-amplitude** (theorem, [source](#stmt-amp-amplitude)): In the finite Koszul specialization at $[t]$ of $\mathsf K(V_\mu^{\boxtimes d})$, retain the part with maximal absolute value for the $d$-fold slide at the chosen leg. Its cohomological upper bound is $$\label{amp:amplitude-bound} C+\max_{e\in E_t}d\langle\mu,h_e\rangle.$$ The constant is independent of $m$. It is also uniform when $t$ is replaced by $tz$, with $z\in Z(\widehat G)^\circ$, after omitting the absolute-value central part, and when one chooses among a fixed finite list of hyperspecial variants. Fixed additional kernels at disjoint places are permitted.* *Unless $t$, modulo its absolute-value central part, is a compact factor times a Jacobson–Morozov point for $Q$, the difference between $2d\langle\lambda,\mu\rangle$ and the nonconstant term in Equation <a href="#amp:amplitude-bound" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="amp:amplitude-bound">[amp:amplitude-bound]</a> is positive and grows linearly with $m$.
  - Explanation: The extreme part of the specialized spherical trace has a cohomological upper bound of constant plus a maximum over the resonant space, and a strictly growing gap unless the parameter is compact times Jacobson-Morozov.
- **claim-rot-trace** (theorem, [source](#stmt-rot-trace)): Let $u$ satisfy the chamber, divisibility, degree-zero, and genericity conditions above. Let $v$ be any fixed regular positive tuple in $\Lambda_0^r$, without a parity restriction. For all sufficiently large integers $n$, $$\label{rot:normalized-trace} \sum_{[\mathcal E]}(T_{nu+v})_{\mathcal E,\mathcal E} =\operatorname{Tr}_{\mathrm{alt}} (\mathrm{Fr}^{n}T_v\mid M(u)).$$ The diagonal sum uses ordinary matrix entries on the rational isomorphism classes of bundles with level, and is finite. The assertion retains arbitrary multiplicities in $D$ and the allowed central quotient.
  - Explanation: For large iterates, the diagonal sum of Bernstein translation operators on rational bundles equals an alternating trace of Frobenius on the cohomology of the translation shtuka.
- **claim-rot-bounded** (theorem, [source](#stmt-rot-bounded)): Under Equation <a href="#rot:genericity" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="rot:genericity">[rot:genericity]</a>, $Y_u$ is a separated Deligne–Mumford stack of finite type, and $C(u)$ is a bounded complex with finite-dimensional cohomology. For a fixed regular positive $v\in\Lambda_0^r$ and all sufficiently large integers $n$, the ordinary rational loop groupoid of exact position $nu+v$ has finitely many isomorphism classes and finite mass. Only bundles of instability $O(1+n)$ occur in it.
  - Explanation: Under genericity the open translation shtuka stack is separated, Deligne-Mumford and of finite type, and rational loop groupoids are finite with bounded instability.
- **claim-rot-finite-correspondence** (theorem, [source](#stmt-rot-finite-correspondence)): The two projections of $\mathcal C_v$ are finite étale after passing to perfections. On $C(u)$ the function-normalized categorical translation is $$\label{rot:geometric-operator} T_v=q^{-L_v/2}p_!q^*.$$ These correspondences compose according to addition in a closed chamber. For $v=u$ the correspondence is the graph of point Frobenius, so $T_u$ acts as Frobenius on $M(u)$.
  - Explanation: Translation operators are given by finite etale correspondences on perfections, composing by addition of translations; the full translation is Frobenius.
- **claim-rot-concatenation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-rot-concatenation)): For sufficiently large $n$, the fixed-point groupoid for $\mathrm{Fr}^{n*}U_v$ on $Y_u$ is equivalent to $\mathcal L_{nu+v}^{\mathrm{sh}}$. Consequently $$\label{rot:shifted-trace} \operatorname{Tr}_{\mathrm{alt}}(\mathrm{Fr}^{n*}U_v\mid C(u)) =\|\mathcal L_{nu+v}^{\mathrm{sh}}\|.$$
  - Explanation: The fixed-point groupoid of the iterated correspondence is the shifted loop groupoid, giving the trace as its weighted cardinality.
- **claim-rot-anisotropic** (theorem, [source](#stmt-rot-anisotropic)): If $a$ satisfies Equation <a href="#rot:genericity" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="rot:genericity">[rot:genericity]</a>, with $a$ replacing $u$, then $S_c$ has no split cocharacter directions outside the connected center of $G$.
  - Explanation: Under the nonvanishing condition the centralizer torus of a loop has no split cocharacters outside the connected center.
- **claim-rot-picard** (theorem, [source](#stmt-rot-picard)): Suppose $a=nu+v$ is regular and satisfies the nonvanishing condition, and suppose the two fixed-point groupoids in question are finite. Then ordinary and shifted Frobenius descent on $\mathcal L_a$ have the same weighted count: $$\label{rot:descent-count} \|\mathcal L_a(\mathbb F_q)\|=\|\mathcal L_a^{\mathrm{sh}}\|.$$ The assertion holds with arbitrary multiplicities in $D$ and after the central quotient described above.
  - Explanation: Ordinary and shifted Frobenius descent on regular loops have equal weighted counts, proved by a connected generalized Picard group action and Lang's theorem.
- **claim-ext-discrete** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ext-discrete)): Let $G$ be split connected reductive, let $x$ be a closed point, and impose Iwahori level at $x$ and arbitrary fixed compact open level away from $x$. Let $\pi$ be a unitary discrete automorphic representation with nonzero invariants at that level. If an ordered Bernstein weight $t_0$ of $\pi_x$ has dominant exponent $\lambda_0=\log_{q_x}\|t_0\|$, then $$\label{ext:local-form} t_0\sim \phi\begin{pmatrix}q_x^{1/2}&0\\0&q_x^{-1/2}\end{pmatrix}c,$$ where $\phi\colon\operatorname{SL}_2\to\widehat G$ is a homomorphism and $c$ is semisimple and conjugate into a maximal compact subgroup of $Z_{\widehat G}(\phi)$.
  - Explanation: A dominant ordered Bernstein weight of a unitary discrete automorphic representation is a compact factor times a Jacobson-Morozov value.
- **claim-ext-hecke** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ext-hecke)): Suppose that $t$ satisfies Equation <a href="#ext:dominant-character" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="ext:dominant-character">[ext:dominant-character]</a>. Then, in $B$, $$\label{ext:hecke-ideals} Bp_tB=\sum_{v\in\mathcal V}Be_vB.$$ Equivalently, a simple module at the central character $Wt$ contains the ordered Bernstein weight $t$ if and only if at least one of the hyperspecial idempotents acts nontrivially on it.
  - Explanation: For a dominant character the ideal of the weight idempotent equals the ideal generated by the hyperspecial idempotents.
- **claim-ext-hecke-finite** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ext-hecke-finite)): In the notation of Lemma <a href="#ext:hecke" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="ext:hecke">26</a>, let $N=\bigoplus_i N^i$ be a graded $B$-module whose $B$-action preserves degree. No finite-generation hypothesis on $N$ is imposed. If $p_tN$ is finite-dimensional, then $e_vN$ is finite-dimensional for every $v\in\mathcal V$. Moreover, $$p_tN^i\ne0\quad\Longrightarrow\quad e_vN^i\ne0\text{ for some }v\in\mathcal V.$$ These assertions apply in particular to any invariant direct summand selected by a projector commuting with $B$.
  - Explanation: Finite-dimensionality and nonvanishing of the weight part of a graded module pass to the hyperspecial parts.
- **claim-ext-spectral** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ext-spectral)): At a fixed compact open level the following assertions hold.* 1. *The discrete summand $\mathcal D_K$ of $\mathcal L_K$ is finite-dimensional. Its orthogonal complement admits a Hecke-equivariant unitary realization as a closed invariant subspace of a finite sum of direct integrals of normalized parabolic inductions $$\operatorname{Ind}_{P(\mathbb A_F)}^{G(\mathbb A_F)} (\sigma\otimes\chi),$$ where $P$ is proper, $\sigma$ belongs to the discrete spectrum of its Levi $M$, and $\chi$ ranges over a compact torus of unitary unramified characters, subject to the central condition imposed by $\Xi$. All fixed-level fiber dimensions are bounded.* 2. *Let $Q_b$ be the orthogonal projection onto the point basis in an HN ball of radius $b$. There are constants $C,c>0$ such that $$\label{ext:discrete-tail} \\|(1-Q_b)v\\|\le C e^{-cb}\\|v\\| \qquad(v\in\mathcal D_K, b\ge0).$$* 3. *Suppose that the level at $x$ is Iwahori. The ordered Bernstein weights of a fiber induced from $M$ have the form $$\label{ext:induced-weights} w(t_M z),\qquad w\Phi_M^+\subset\Phi_G^+,$$ where $t_M$ is an ordered Bernstein weight of $\sigma_x$ and $z$ is the local value of $\chi$. There are finitely many such weight families, and their generalized-weight lengths are uniformly bounded.
  - Explanation: At fixed level the discrete spectrum is finite dimensional, the complement is realized by induced families, discrete mass decays exponentially outside balls, and induced weights have a controlled form.
- **claim-ext-matrix-bound** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ext-matrix-bound)): Let $B,r_0>0$ and $R_0\in\mathbf N$ be fixed. For every $r>r_0$ there is a constant $C$ with the following property: if $A$ is an operator on a Hilbert space of dimension at most $R_0$, with $\\|A\\|\le B$ and all eigenvalues of modulus at most $r_0$, then $$\\|A^n\\|\le Cr^n\qquad(n\ge0).$$
  - Explanation: Powers of operators of bounded dimension and norm grow no faster than any rate exceeding the spectral radius bound, with a uniform constant.
- **claim-ext-spectral-gap** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ext-spectral-gap)): There is a Laurent polynomial $P_0$ in the degree-zero Bernstein translations at $x$, nonzero at $t_0$, and an open cone of dominant regular directions arbitrarily close to $\lambda_0$, such that the following holds. For every fixed auxiliary label $\zeta$ at $y$ and every sufficiently large label $\mu$ in this cone, every continuous joint weight surviving $P_0$ has strictly smaller modulus on $T_{(\mu,\zeta)}$ than the target joint weight.
  - Explanation: A polynomial filter and a cone of directions make all surviving continuous weights strictly smaller in modulus than the target weight.
- **claim-ext-lower** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ext-lower)): Under the induction assumption and the choice of a violating weight above, let $u=(\mu,\zeta)$, where $\mu$ is sufficiently large in the cone of Lemma <a href="#ext:spectral-gap" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="ext:spectral-gap">30</a> and $u$ satisfies Equation <a href="#rot:genericity" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="rot:genericity">[rot:genericity]</a>. Then $M(u)$ contains the joint Bernstein character $(t_0,t_{0,y})$ on $(L^0)^2$. At some full ordered extension $(t_x,t_y)$ of that character it has nonzero cohomology in a degree $i$ satisfying $$\label{ext:degree-lower} i\ge 2d_x\langle\lambda_0,\mu\rangle +2\log_q\|\zeta(t_{0,y})\|.$$
  - Explanation: The translation shtuka cohomology contains the target joint character, in a degree bounded below by a Frobenius weight comparison.
- **claim-ext-rationality** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ext-rationality)): The semisimple exponent $\lambda_0$ of a violating weight in this induction argument is rational.
  - Explanation: Integrality of Frobenius weights forces the semisimple exponent of a violating weight to be rational.
- **claim-ext-unique-extreme** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ext-unique-extreme)): For $\mu$ as in Equation <a href="#ext:exact-ray" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="ext:exact-ray">[ext:exact-ray]</a>, the only weight $a$ of the irreducible representation $V_\mu$ maximizing $\langle\lambda_0,a\rangle$ is $a=\mu$, and its multiplicity is one. Consequently the only maximizing tuple in $V_\mu^{\otimes d_x}$ is $(\mu,\ldots,\mu)$.
  - Explanation: On the exact ray the highest weight is the unique maximizer of the exponent pairing, with multiplicity one.
- **claim-ext-extreme-transfer** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ext-extreme-transfer)): Fix a positive even integer $\kappa$ removing the slide convention signs, and let $S_x$ be the $\kappa$th power of the $d$-fold $x$-label slide. There is a polynomial projector $\varepsilon_{\mathrm{ext}}$ on $\widehat H_\mu$, commuting with $\mathcal H_x\otimes_{Z_x}R_x$, such that every $$N_v=e_v\varepsilon_{\mathrm{ext}}\widehat H_\mu$$ has finite-dimensional total graded space. For some hyperspecial vertex $v$, it is nonzero in a degree satisfying Equation <a href="#ext:degree-lower" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="ext:degree-lower">[ext:degree-lower]</a>. The transfer identifies $N_v$ with the extreme $S_x$-part of $H^*(C_{\mu,v}^{\mathrm{sph}})\otimes_{Z_x}R_x$, where $C_{\mu,v}^{\mathrm{sph}} =\mathsf K_v(V_\mu^{\boxtimes d};J_\zeta)$.
  - Explanation: A polynomial projector isolates the extreme slide part of the Iwahori cohomology and transfers it to spherical cohomology with finite-dimensional total space.
- **claim-ext-spherical-projector** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ext-spherical-projector)): For each $v$, the genuine spherical $Z_x$-dg module $C_{\mu,v}^{\mathrm{sph}}$ has, after flat scalar extension to $R_x$, a dg direct summand $E_{\mu,v}$ with $H^*(E_{\mu,v})=N_v$. Its chain projector is polynomial in the spherical slide $S_x$ and commutes with finite Koszul tests over $R_x$.
  - Explanation: The spherical dg module has a genuine dg direct summand realizing the extreme cohomology, with a chain projector polynomial in the slide.
- **claim-ext-koszul** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ext-koszul)): Let $(R,\mathfrak m)$ be a noetherian local ring and let $C$ be a complex of $R$-modules bounded above in cohomology. Suppose that $N$ is its highest nonzero cohomological degree and $H^N(C)$ is a finite nonzero $R$-module. If $f_1,\ldots,f_r$ generate $\mathfrak m$, then $$H^N\bigl(C\otimes_R K_R(f_1,\ldots,f_r)\bigr)\ne0,$$ where the Koszul complex is placed in degrees $[-r,0]$. No regular-sequence hypothesis is required.
  - Explanation: The top nonzero cohomology of a bounded-above complex survives tensoring with a Koszul complex on generators of the maximal ideal.
- **claim-glob-hecke-spectrum** (theorem, [source](#stmt-glob-hecke-spectrum)): The algebra $\mathbb T_{D,\mathbb Q}$ is finite dimensional and reduced. After extending scalars to $\overline{\mathbb Q}$, its action has a decomposition $$\label{glob:joint-spectrum} \overline{\mathbb Q}\otimes_{\mathbb Q}C_{D,\mathbb Q} =\bigoplus_{\chi\in\Sigma_D} C_\chi$$ into joint eigenspaces, where $\Sigma_D$ is finite. All good-place Satake classes of these characters are algebraic.
  - Explanation: The Hecke algebra on the rational cusp space is finite dimensional and reduced, giving a decomposition into joint eigenspaces with algebraic Satake classes.
- **claim-glob-embeddings** (theorem, [source](#stmt-glob-embeddings)): Suppose that, at every embedding $\iota$, the element $\iota(t)$ is conjugate to $$\phi_\iota\!\begin{pmatrix}\sqrt r&0\\0&\sqrt r^{-1}\end{pmatrix} c_\iota,$$ where $\phi_\iota:\operatorname{SL}_2\to\widehat G_{\mathbb C}$ is an algebraic homomorphism and $c_\iota$ is semisimple and conjugate into a compact subgroup of $Z_{\widehat G}(\phi_\iota(\operatorname{SL}_2))(\mathbb C)$. Then the following hold.* 1. *The vectors $\lambda_\iota(t)$ are all equal to a vector $\lambda$, and $\mu=2\lambda$ belongs to $X_*(T)$.* 2. *There is a homomorphism $\phi:\operatorname{SL}_2\to\widehat G_{\overline{\mathbb Q}}$ with diagonal cocharacter $\mu$ such that $$t=\mu(\sqrt r)c, \qquad c\in Z_{\widehat G}(\phi(\operatorname{SL}_2)) (\overline{\mathbb Q}),$$ and the image of $c$ is compact at every complex embedding.* 3. *The nilpotent orbit of the raising element of $\phi$ is unique. In particular, $[t]$ belongs to precisely one of the sets $R_{r,\mathcal O}$.
  - Explanation: Compact-Jacobson-Morozov form at all embeddings forces a common integral neutral cocharacter, an algebraic triple with compact remainder, and a unique nilpotent orbit.
- **claim-glob-signs** (theorem, [source](#stmt-glob-signs)): The sets $R_{r,\mathcal O}$ are pairwise disjoint by Lemma <a href="#glob:embeddings" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="glob:embeddings">38</a>. They are Galois-stable with the rational forms stipulated in their definition (Gaitsgory, Lafforgue, and Raskin 2026, sec. 3.4.2) [[GLR](#ref-GLR)]. To see the square-root issue directly, replacing $\sqrt r$ by $-\sqrt r$ inserts $\phi_{\mathcal O}(-I)$ into the compact factor. This element is central in the triple centralizer and has finite order, so preserves compactness at every embedding. Multiplication of $t$ by any finite-order element of $Z(\widehat G)$ likewise preserves its orbit label and membership in $R_{r,\mathcal O}$. For later use, let $2\rho_G$ be the sum of the positive roots of $G$, viewed as a cocharacter of the dual torus. In the usual square-root presentation of normalized Satake, if $\tau$ changes the chosen square root of $q_x$ by a sign $\epsilon_{\tau,x}$, then the Satake class of the Galois-conjugate Hecke character is $$\label{glob:satake-galois} [t_{\chi^\tau,x}] =[(2\rho_G)(\epsilon_{\tau,x})\,\tau(t_{\chi,x})].$$ Indeed, on a dominant coweight $a$ the change of the normalization factor is $\epsilon_{\tau,x}^{\langle2\rho_G,a\rangle}$. The element $(2\rho_G)(-1)$ is central in $\widehat G$, since its pairing with every dual root is even. It has order at most two. Thus Equation <a href="#glob:satake-galois" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="glob:satake-galois">[glob:satake-galois]</a> preserves all the assertions of Lemma <a href="#glob:embeddings" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="glob:embeddings">38</a>. If the rational Satake form absorbs this correction, the comparison is ordinary Galois equivariance. In either presentation of the identification in (Gaitsgory, Lafforgue, and Raskin 2026, Equation (3.2)) [[GLR](#ref-GLR)], the comparison differs at most by the finite central factor just computed; only this conclusion is used below.
  - Explanation: The orbit support sets are pairwise disjoint and Galois stable, and sign choices of square roots change only by a finite central element.
- **claim-glob-twist-purity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-glob-twist-purity)): Let $\mathcal V$ be a lisse $\overline{\mathbb Q}_\ell$-sheaf on the smooth geometrically connected curve $U/\mathbb F_q$, defined over a finite extension of $\mathbb Q_\ell$. Suppose that all its Frobenius eigenvalues at closed points are algebraic over $\mathbb Q$. For each complex embedding of these algebraic numbers, the multiset of numbers $$\log_{q_x}\|\alpha\|, \qquad \alpha\text{ an eigenvalue of } \operatorname{Frob}_x\text{ on }\mathcal V,$$ counted with multiplicity, is independent of $x\in\|U\|$.
  - Explanation: For a lisse sheaf with algebraic Frobenius eigenvalues on the good open curve, the multiset of logarithmic absolute values is independent of the closed point.
- **claim-glob-place** (theorem, [source](#stmt-glob-place)): Fix a joint character $\chi\in\Sigma_D$ and an embedding $\iota:\overline{\mathbb Q}\hookrightarrow\mathbb C$. Let $\lambda_{\chi,x}$ be the dominant exponent of its normalized Satake class at $x\in\|U\|$, with base $q_x$ and absolute value induced by $\iota$. Then $\lambda_{\chi,x}$ is independent of $x$.
  - Explanation: The dominant exponent of the Satake class of a joint Hecke character does not depend on the good place.
- **claim-glob-descent** (theorem, [source](#stmt-glob-descent)): For every effective divisor $D$, the rational cuspidal space has the decomposition $$\label{glob:rational-decomposition} C_{D,\mathbb Q} =\bigoplus_{\mathcal O}C_{D,\mathcal O,\mathbb Q}$$ with the spectral-support summands specified in the introduction.
  - Explanation: Galois-stable orbit partitions give rational idempotents, so the rational cusp space is the direct sum of the orbit summands.

      "exact_statement": "For every $X,G,D,\\ell$ as above, the natural addition maps $$\\label{intro:decomposition}\n \\bigoplus_{\\mathcal O}C_{D,\\mathcal O,\\mathbb Q}\n       \\xrightarrow{\\ \\sim\\ }C_{D,\\mathbb Q},\n \\qquad\n \\bigoplus_{\\mathcal O}C_{D,\\mathcal O,\\ell}\n       \\xrightarrow{\\ \\sim\\ }C_{D,\\ell}$$ are isomorphisms.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-main-generic-ramanujan",
        "exact_quote": "Let $F$ be the function field of a smooth projective geometrically connected curve over a finite field, and let $G/F$ be split connected adjoint absolutely simple. Let $\\pi=\\bigotimes'_x\\pi_x$ be a complex cuspidal automorphic representation of $G(\\mathbb A_F)$. If $\\pi_v$ is unramified and generic at one place $v$, then $\\pi_x$ is tempered at every place $x$ at which it is unramified. In particular, every globally generic cuspidal automorphic representation of $G(\\mathbb A_F)$ is tempered at every unramified place.",
        "source_id": "PM-014.3"
      },
      "evidence": [

          "source_id": "PM-014.3"

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-cat-specialization",
        "exact_quote": "For every prime $p\\ne\\ell$, the geometric characteristic-zero and $\\overline{\\mathbf F}_p$ categories $\\mathscr H_{P,Q}$ admit simultaneous enhanced equivalences preserving standard, costandard, and intersection complexes, convolution, and change of parahoric level. They also preserve restriction of equivariance from $L^+G$ to the pro-unipotent radical $I^u$ of an Iwahori, including the induced maps of mapping complexes. These are assertions about ordinary geometric categories; Weil structures will be specified separately.",
        "source_id": "PM-014.3"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-cat-satake",
        "exact_quote": "The stable idempotent-complete tensor category generated by the normalized spherical Satake complexes is monoidally equivalent to $$\\operatorname{Perf}^{\\widehat G}(S).$$ The normalized Satake object $\\operatorname{Sat}(V)$ corresponds to $S\\otimes V$. With the split Weil structures, Frobenius pullback corresponds to extension of scalars by $$S\\longrightarrow S,\\qquad \\alpha\\longmapsto q\\alpha\n \\quad(\\alpha\\in\\widehat{\\mathfrak g}^*[-2]),$$ and acts trivially on the representation labels. These assertions include the enhanced monoidal structure and all morphisms between the indicated objects.",
        "source_id": "PM-014.3"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-cat-central",
        "exact_quote": "There is a tensor functor to the Drinfeld center of the homotopy category $$Z:\\operatorname{Rep}(\\widehat G)\\longrightarrow\n       \\mathcal Z(\\operatorname{Ho}(\\mathscr H_{I,I}))$$ with its split Weil structure. Its interchanges are compatible with projection to every standard parahoric. At a hyperspecial parahoric the projected functor is Satake.*\n\n*There are invertible kernels $J_a$, $a\\in\\Lambda$, with coherent multiplication $J_a\\star J_b\\simeq J_{a+b}$. They are standard on the positive chamber and costandard on its opposite. The central object $Z(V)$ has a finite functorial Wakimoto filtration, tensor compatible in $V$, with $$\\operatorname{gr}_a Z(V)=J_a\\otimes V_a.$$ Its induced central interchange with $J_b$ is the ordinary interchange of the two translation factors and the identity on the multiplicity space, with the fixed Satake parity convention. The multiplicity spaces have trivial semisimplified Frobenius. These statements hold over every $\\mathbf F_q$ under consideration. For a fixed central label, its interchange in the other kernel variable is a chosen natural equivalence of exact enhanced functors. We use the central tensor identities in the homotopy category; we do not assert an enhancement of $Z$ to an infinity-categorical Drinfeld center.",
        "source_id": "PM-014.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-cat-central",
          "exact_quote": "There is a tensor functor to the Drinfeld center of the homotopy category $$Z:\\operatorname{Rep}(\\widehat G)\\longrightarrow\n       \\mathcal Z(\\operatorname{Ho}(\\mathscr H_{I,I}))$$ with its split Weil structure. Its interchanges are compatible with projection to every standard parahoric. At a hyperspecial parahoric the projected functor is Satake.*\n\n*There are invertible kernels $J_a$, $a\\in\\Lambda$, with coherent multiplication $J_a\\star J_b\\simeq J_{a+b}$. The multiplicity spaces have trivial semisimplified Frobenius. These statements hold over every $\\mathbf F_q$ under consideration. For a fixed central label, its interchange in the other kernel variable is a chosen natural equivalence of exact enhanced functors. We use the central tensor identities in the homotopy category; we do not assert an enhancement of $Z$ to an infinity-categorical Drinfeld center.",
          "source_id": "PM-014.3"

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-cat-springer",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\operatorname{For}$ forget $L^+G$-equivariance to $I^u$-equivariance on $\\operatorname{Gr}_G$, without changing the underlying complex, and let $\\operatorname{Av}_*$ be its right adjoint. Put $W_\\eta=J_\\eta\\star\\delta_0$. For every integral weight $\\eta$ in the closed costandard chamber, derived Satake identifies $$\\operatorname{Av}_*W_\\eta\n \\quad\\text{with}\\quad\n \\bigl(R\\pi_*\\mathcal O_{\\widetilde{\\mathcal N}}(\\eta)\\bigr)_{\\rm sh},\n \\qquad\n \\pi:\\widetilde{\\mathcal N}\\longrightarrow\\widehat{\\mathfrak g}.$$ The subscript means that coherent degree $a$ and polynomial degree $b$ are assigned total degree $a+2b$. This is an equivalence of ordinary equivariant dg $S$-modules. The $S$-action is polynomial restriction along $\\pi$, and the assertion is compatible with tensoring by representations and with all morphisms between free Satake labels. The displayed convention introduces no extra shift. Any smooth-pullback normalization of $\\operatorname{For}$ changes the formula only by a fixed shift and Tate twist, independent of $\\eta$.",
        "source_id": "PM-014.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-cat-springer",
          "exact_quote": "Let $\\operatorname{For}$ forget $L^+G$-equivariance to $I^u$-equivariance on $\\operatorname{Gr}_G$, without changing the underlying complex, and let $\\operatorname{Av}_*$ be its right adjoint. For every integral weight $\\eta$ in the closed costandard chamber, derived Satake identifies $$\\operatorname{Av}_*W_\\eta\n \\quad\\text{with}\\quad\n \\bigl(R\\pi_*\\mathcal O_{\\widetilde{\\mathcal N}}(\\eta)\\bigr)_{\\rm sh},\n \\qquad\n \\pi:\\widetilde{\\mathcal N}\\longrightarrow\\widehat{\\mathfrak g}.$$ The subscript means that coherent degree $a$ and polynomial degree $b$ are assigned total degree $a+2b$. This is an equivalence of ordinary equivariant dg $S$-modules. The $S$-action is polynomial restriction along $\\pi$, and the assertion is compatible with tensoring by representations and with all morphisms between free Satake labels. The displayed convention introduces no extra shift. Any smooth-pullback normalization of $\\operatorname{For}$ changes the formula only by a fixed shift and Tate twist, independent of $\\eta$.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-cat-cyclic",
        "exact_quote": "The preceding construction is an exact enhanced Frobenius-twisted cyclic functor. Put $\\Phi=\\mathrm{Fr}^*$ on local kernels. With convolution ordered in the direction of the path, its cyclic maps are $$\\label{cat:last-first}\n \\rho_{U,V}:\\mathsf K(U\\star V)\n       \\xrightarrow{\\sim}\\mathsf K(\\Phi(V)\\star U).$$ They are natural in all enhanced morphisms and coherently compatible with units, iterated products, and change of level. Here $\\rho_{U,\\mathbf1}$ is the identity. The full slide $\\rho_{\\mathbf1,V}:\\mathsf K(V)\\to\\mathsf K(\\Phi V)$, followed by the Weil structure of $V$, is cohomological Frobenius. Slides at disjoint sets of legs commute; their product is the full slide.",
        "source_id": "PM-014.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-cat-cyclic",
          "exact_quote": "The preceding construction is an exact enhanced Frobenius-twisted cyclic functor. Put $\\Phi=\\mathrm{Fr}^*$ on local kernels. Here $\\rho_{U,\\mathbf1}$ is the identity. The full slide $\\rho_{\\mathbf1,V}:\\mathsf K(V)\\to\\mathsf K(\\Phi V)$, followed by the Weil structure of $V$, is cohomological Frobenius. Slides at disjoint sets of legs commute; their product is the full slide.",
          "source_id": "PM-014.3"

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-cat-hecke",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose that $L$ has a central label at a closed place $x$. The operators in Equation <a href=\"#cat:character\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"cat:character\">[cat:character]</a> give the usual affine Hecke algebra at $x$, with parameter $q_x$, on $H^\\bullet\\mathsf K(L)$. They give ordinary Hecke operators on $H^\\bullet\\mathsf K(\\mathbf1)$.*\n\n\n*More explicitly, let $H_w$ denote the operator with characteristic function $1_{IwI}$ and Iwahori volume one. Then $$H_s^2=(q_x-1)H_s+q_x,\n \\qquad H_wH_{w'}=H_{ww'}\\quad\n       \\text{if }\\ell(ww')=\\ell(w)+\\ell(w').$$ The positive normalized Bernstein translations are $$\\label{cat:translation-sign}\n T_a=(-1)^{\\ell(t^a)}\\chi_{J_a},\n \\qquad\n T_a=q_x^{-\\ell(t^a)/2}H_{t^a}\\quad(a\\text{ positive}),$$ and extend multiplicatively to the entire translation lattice.*\n\n*For a standard parahoric $P\\supset I$, its two level transfers identify the hyperspecial or parahoric trace cohomology with the image of $$e_P=\\left(\\sum_{w\\in W_P}q_x^{\\ell(w)}\\right)^{-1}\n                  \\sum_{w\\in W_P}H_w.$$ At hyperspecial level the spherical character action agrees with the Bernstein center. The individual-leg slides commute with these actions and transfers. On the Wakimoto graded terms the Bernstein action is the translation action tensored with the weight multiplicities in Theorem <a href=\"#cat:central\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"cat:central\">5</a>. The Wakimoto filtration gives long exact sequences equivariant for these Bernstein translations and for the selected-leg slides. Its graded terms carry the stated translation action. Thus flat completion, ordered-weight projections, and spectral projectors may be applied to these cohomology sequences.*\n\n*There is also the following geometric form of the rank-one assertion. If an exact translation position is parallel to the simple wall of $s$, the operator $H_s+1$ on its path trace is pullback and sum along the finite etale family of Frobenius-fixed rank-one refinement flags, of degree $q_x+1$. Consequently $H_s$ takes the sum over the other flags.",
        "source_id": "PM-014.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-cat-hecke",
          "exact_quote": "Suppose that $L$ has a central label at a closed place $x$. They give ordinary Hecke operators on $H^\\bullet\\mathsf K(\\mathbf1)$.*\n\n\n*More explicitly, let $H_w$ denote the operator with characteristic function $1_{IwI}$ and Iwahori volume one. Then $$H_s^2=(q_x-1)H_s+q_x,\n \\qquad H_wH_{w'}=H_{ww'}\\quad\n       \\text{if }\\ell(ww')=\\ell(w)+\\ell(w').$$ The positive normalized Bernstein translations are $$\\label{cat:translation-sign}\n T_a=(-1)^{\\ell(t^a)}\\chi_{J_a},\n \\qquad\n T_a=q_x^{-\\ell(t^a)/2}H_{t^a}\\quad(a\\text{ positive}),$$ and extend multiplicatively to the entire translation lattice.*\n\n*For a standard parahoric $P\\supset I$, its two level transfers identify the hyperspecial or parahoric trace cohomology with the image of $$e_P=\\left(\\sum_{w\\in W_P}q_x^{\\ell(w)}\\right)^{-1}\n                  \\sum_{w\\in W_P}H_w.$$ At hyperspecial level the spherical character action agrees with the Bernstein center.       "exact_statement": "Suppose that $L$ has a central label at a closed place $x$. The operators in Equation <a href=\"#cat:character\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"cat:character\">[cat:character]</a> give the usual affine Hecke algebra at $x$, with parameter $q_x$, on $H^\\bullet\\mathsf K(L)$. They give ordinary Hecke operators on $H^\\bullet\\mathsf K(\\mathbf1)$.*\n\n\n*More explicitly, let $H_w$ denote the operator with characteristic function $1_{IwI}$ and Iwahori volume one. Then $$H_s^2=(q_x-1)H_s+q_x,\n \\qquad H_wH_{w'}=H_{ww'}\\quad\n       \\text{if }\\ell(ww')=\\ell(w)+\\ell(w').$$ The positive normalized Bernstein translations are $$\\label{cat:translation-sign}\n T_a=(-1)^{\\ell(t^a)}\\chi_{J_a},\n \\qquad\n T_a=q_x^{-\\ell(t^a)/2}H_{t^a}\\quad(a\\text{ positive}),$$ and extend multiplicatively to the entire translation lattice.*\n\n*For a standard parahoric $P\\supset I$, its two level transfers identify the hyperspecial or parahoric trace cohomology with the image of $$e_P=\\left(\\sum_{w\\in W_P}q_x^{\\ell(w)}\\right)^{-1}\n                  \\sum_{w\\in W_P}H_w.$$ At hyperspecial level the spherical character action agrees with the Bernstein center. The individual-leg slides commute with these actions and transfers. On the Wakimoto graded terms the Bernstein action is the translation action tensored with the weight multiplicities in Theorem <a href=\"#cat:central\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"cat:central\">5</a>. The Wakimoto filtration gives long exact sequences equivariant for these Bernstein translations and for the selected-leg slides. Its graded terms carry the stated translation action. Thus flat completion, ordered-weight projections, and spectral projectors may be applied to these cohomology sequences.*\n\n*There is also the following geometric form of the rank-one assertion. If an exact translation position is parallel to the simple wall of $s$, the operator $H_s+1$ on its path trace is pullback and sum along the finite etale family of Frobenius-fixed rank-one refinement flags, of degree $q_x+1$. Consequently $H_s$ takes the sum over the other flags.",
      "explanation": "Character operators from rigid kernels realize the affine Hecke algebra on the cohomology of the trace, with Bernstein translations, parahoric transfers and a rank-one flag description.",

        "anchor": "stmt-cat-loops",
        "exact_quote": "At a hyperspecial closed-place leg of degree $d$, the functor $\\mathsf K$ on perfect spherical labels has the form $$\\label{cat:loop-pairing}\n \\mathsf K(P)\\simeq\n \\left(\\mathcal A\\otimes_{B_{q^d}}\n                  (B_{q^d}\\otimes_{S^{\\otimes d}}P)\\right)^{\\widehat G},$$ where $\\mathcal A$ is an equivariant dg module, with no coherence or boundedness assumption. After eliminating intermediate factors, the algebra is $$\\label{cat:loop-algebra}\n B_r=\\mathcal O(\\widehat G)\\otimes\n       \\operatorname{Sym}(\\widehat{\\mathfrak g}^*[-2]\\oplus\\widehat{\\mathfrak g}^*[-1]),\n \\qquad r=q^d.$$ Write $s$ for its degree-zero holonomy variable and $e$ for its degree-two linear variable. The second summand supplies odd Koszul generators $\\eta_\\alpha$ of degree $1$, with $$\\label{cat:holonomy}\n d\\eta_\\alpha=\n       \\langle\\alpha,\\operatorname{Ad}(s)e-q^d e\\rangle.$$ The group acts by simultaneous conjugation.*\n\n*The $d$-fold selected-leg slide on representation labels is the action of $s$ on each factor. Spherical Hecke characters act by the corresponding invariant characters of $s$. On cohomology these descriptions agree with the transfers in Proposition <a href=\"#cat:hecke\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"cat:hecke\">8</a>. A degree-two morphism linear in the selected $e$ variable multiplies the corresponding slide eigenvalue by $q^d$. All statements are natural in perfect labels and their dg morphisms.",

        "anchor": "stmt-amp-geometry",
        "exact_quote": "For a fixed split connected reductive group and fixed finite level, the following statements hold.*\n\n1.  *The number of rational isomorphism classes of bundles of instability at most $b$ is bounded by $C(1+b)^N$, for fixed $C,N$.*\n\n2.  *There is a locally finite constructible stratification of the bundle stack such that, on a stratum where the automorphism group has dimension $r$, the stratum has dimension at most $C-r$. The same constant works for all Harder–Narasimhan types.*\n\n3.  *Suppose that a sequence of bundles $\\mathcal E_n$ has meromorphic isomorphisms to its point-Frobenius transforms, or meromorphic automorphisms, with the $n$th modification of size at most $b_n$. If the ratio of the instability of $\\mathcal E_n$ to $1+b_n$ is unbounded, a subsequence has a coarsening of the canonical reduction to one fixed maximal parabolic that these isomorphisms carry to its point-Frobenius transform, or to itself, respectively. The assertion holds over geometric points and is compatible with descent and iteration of the Frobenius isomorphism.*\n\n*All statements allow an arbitrary effective level divisor, including nonreduced divisors, and any fixed list of parahoric flags.",
        "source_id": "PM-014.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-amp-geometry",

The same constant works for all Harder–Narasimhan types.*\n\n3.  *Suppose that a sequence of bundles $\\mathcal E_n$ has meromorphic isomorphisms to its point-Frobenius transforms, or meromorphic automorphisms, with the $n$th modification of size at most $b_n$.         "exact_quote": "Let $\\mathcal B$ be one of the bundle stacks in Proposition <a href=\"#amp:geometry\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"amp:geometry\">10</a>.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-amp-amplitude",
        "exact_quote": "In the finite Koszul specialization at $[t]$ of $\\mathsf K(V_\\mu^{\\boxtimes d})$, retain the part with maximal absolute value for the $d$-fold slide at the chosen leg. Its cohomological upper bound is $$\\label{amp:amplitude-bound}\n C+\\max_{e\\in E_t}d\\langle\\mu,h_e\\rangle.$$ The constant is independent of $m$. It is also uniform when $t$ is replaced by $tz$, with $z\\in Z(\\widehat G)^\\circ$, after omitting the absolute-value central part, and when one chooses among a fixed finite list of hyperspecial variants. Fixed additional kernels at disjoint places are permitted.*\n\n*Unless $t$, modulo its absolute-value central part, is a compact factor times a Jacobson–Morozov point for $Q$, the difference between $2d\\langle\\lambda,\\mu\\rangle$ and the nonconstant term in Equation <a href=\"#amp:amplitude-bound\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"amp:amplitude-bound\">[amp:amplitude-bound]</a> is positive and grows linearly with $m$.",

        "anchor": "stmt-ext-discrete",
        "exact_quote": "Let $G$ be split connected reductive, let $x$ be a closed point, and impose Iwahori level at $x$ and arbitrary fixed compact open level away from $x$.         "source_id": "PM-014.3"
      },

          "source_id": "PM-014.3"

        "anchor": "stmt-ext-hecke",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose that $t$ satisfies Equation <a href=\"#ext:dominant-character\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"ext:dominant-character\">[ext:dominant-character]</a>. Then, in $B$, $$\\label{ext:hecke-ideals}\n    Bp_tB=\\sum_{v\\in\\mathcal V}Be_vB.$$ Equivalently, a simple module at the central character $Wt$ contains the ordered Bernstein weight $t$ if and only if at least one of the hyperspecial idempotents acts nontrivially on it.",
        "source_id": "PM-014.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-ext-hecke",
          "exact_quote": "Suppose that $t$ satisfies Equation <a href=\"#ext:dominant-character\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"ext:dominant-character\">[ext:dominant-character]</a>. Then, in $B$, $$\\label{ext:hecke-ideals}\n    Bp_tB=\\sum_{v\\in\\mathcal V}Be_vB.$$ Equivalently, a simple module at the central character $Wt$ contains the ordered Bernstein weight $t$ if and only if at least one of the hyperspecial idempotents acts nontrivially on it.",
          "source_id": "PM-014.3"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Suppose that $t$ satisfies Equation <a href=\"#ext:dominant-character\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"ext:dominant-character\">[ext:dominant-character]</a>.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-ext-hecke-finite",
        "exact_quote": "In the notation of Lemma <a href=\"#ext:hecke\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"ext:hecke\">26</a>, let $N=\\bigoplus_i N^i$ be a graded $B$-module whose $B$-action preserves degree. No finite-generation hypothesis on $N$ is imposed. If $p_tN$ is finite-dimensional, then $e_vN$ is finite-dimensional for every $v\\in\\mathcal V$. Moreover, $$p_tN^i\\ne0\\quad\\Longrightarrow\\quad\n    e_vN^i\\ne0\\text{ for some }v\\in\\mathcal V.$$ These assertions apply in particular to any invariant direct summand selected by a projector commuting with $B$.",
        "source_id": "PM-014.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-ext-hecke-finite",
          "exact_quote": "In the notation of Lemma <a href=\"#ext:hecke\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"ext:hecke\">26</a>, let $N=\\bigoplus_i N^i$ be a graded $B$-module whose $B$-action preserves degree. No finite-generation hypothesis on $N$ is imposed. If $p_tN$ is finite-dimensional, then $e_vN$ is finite-dimensional for every $v\\in\\mathcal V$. Moreover, $$p_tN^i\\ne0\\quad\\Longrightarrow\\quad\n    e_vN^i\\ne0\\text{ for some }v\\in\\mathcal V.$$ These assertions apply in particular to any invariant direct summand selected by a projector commuting with $B$.",
          "source_id": "PM-014.3"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "In the notation of Lemma <a href=\"#ext:hecke\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"ext:hecke\">26</a>, let $N=\\bigoplus_i N^i$ be a graded $B$-module whose $B$-action preserves degree.         "anchor": "stmt-ext-spectral",
        "exact_quote": "At a fixed compact open level the following assertions hold.*\n\n1.  *The discrete summand $\\mathcal D_K$ of $\\mathcal L_K$ is finite-dimensional. Its orthogonal complement admits a Hecke-equivariant unitary realization as a closed invariant subspace of a finite sum of direct integrals of normalized parabolic inductions $$\\operatorname{Ind}_{P(\\mathbb A_F)}^{G(\\mathbb A_F)}\n                     (\\sigma\\otimes\\chi),$$ where $P$ is proper, $\\sigma$ belongs to the discrete spectrum of its Levi $M$, and $\\chi$ ranges over a compact torus of unitary unramified characters, subject to the central condition imposed by $\\Xi$. All fixed-level fiber dimensions are bounded.*\n\n\n2.  *Let $Q_b$ be the orthogonal projection onto the point basis in an HN ball of radius $b$. There are constants $C,c>0$ such that $$\\label{ext:discrete-tail}\n       \\|(1-Q_b)v\\|\\le C e^{-cb}\\|v\\|\n           \\qquad(v\\in\\mathcal D_K, b\\ge0).$$*\n\n\n3.  *Suppose that the level at $x$ is Iwahori.         "source_id": "PM-014.3"

        "anchor": "stmt-ext-extreme-transfer",
        "exact_quote": "Fix a positive even integer $\\kappa$ removing the slide convention signs, and let $S_x$ be the $\\kappa$th power of the $d$-fold $x$-label slide. For some hyperspecial vertex $v$, it is nonzero in a degree satisfying Equation <a href=\"#ext:degree-lower\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"ext:degree-lower\">[ext:degree-lower]</a>. The transfer identifies $N_v$ with the extreme $S_x$-part of $H^*(C_{\\mu,v}^{\\mathrm{sph}})\\otimes_{Z_x}R_x$, where $C_{\\mu,v}^{\\mathrm{sph}}\n   =\\mathsf K_v(V_\\mu^{\\boxtimes d};J_\\zeta)$.",

        "exact_quote": "Let $(R,\\mathfrak m)$ be a noetherian local ring and let $C$ be a complex of $R$-modules bounded above in cohomology.         "exact_quote": "The sets $R_{r,\\mathcal O}$ are pairwise disjoint by Lemma <a href=\"#glob:embeddings\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"glob:embeddings\">38</a>. They are Galois-stable with the rational forms stipulated in their definition (Gaitsgory, Lafforgue, and Raskin 2026, sec. 3.4.2) [[GLR](#ref-GLR)]. To see the square-root issue directly, replacing $\\sqrt r$ by $-\\sqrt r$ inserts $\\phi_{\\mathcal O}(-I)$ into the compact factor. If the rational Satake form absorbs this correction, the comparison is ordinary Galois equivariance. <a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **generic local representation** ([source](#pm-h-4)): A local representation of $G(F_x)$ is *generic* if it admits a nonzero Whittaker functional for a nondegenerate character of $N(F_x)$.
- **globally generic cuspidal representation** ([source](#pm-h-4)): A cuspidal automorphic representation is *globally generic* if one of its vectors has a nonzero global Whittaker coefficient for a nondegenerate character of $N(F)\backslash N(\mathbb A_F)$.
- **ordered Bernstein weight** ([source](#sec:extraction)): An *ordered Bernstein weight* of a local representation at a place $x$ is a character $t_0:X_*(T)\to\mathbf k^\times$ occurring in its Iwahori invariants, before passage to its Weyl orbit.
- **corrected fiber** ([source](#amp:corrected-fiber)): The result is called the *corrected fiber*, denoted $F_e^{\mathrm{corr}}$.

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-linear-degree | cohomological degree of a linear polynomial | = | $2$ | all linear generators |
| q-hecke-quadratic | quadratic relation of simple Hecke generator | = | $(q_x-1)H_s+q_x$ | simple reflection s |
| q-flag-family-degree | degree of the family of fixed rank-one flags | = | $q_x+1$ | place x |
| q-instability-count | number of rational bundle classes of bounded instability | ≤ | $C(1+b)^N$ | instability at most b |
| q-stratum-dimension | dimension of a stratum with automorphism dimension r | ≤ | $C-r$ | automorphism dimension r |
| q-frobenius-graph-bound | upper bound after pullback to the Frobenius graph | ≤ | $N+C_1$ | upper perverse bound N |
| q-extreme-amplitude | cohomological upper bound of the extreme slide part | ≤ | $C+\max_{e\in E_t}d\langle\mu,h_e\rangle$ | constant independent of m |
| q-discrete-tail | discrete mass outside a Harder-Narasimhan ball | ≤ | $C e^{-cb}\\|v\\|$ | radius b |
| q-matrix-power | operator norm of powers | ≤ | $Cr^n$ | r above the eigenvalue bound |
| q-degree-lower | highest nonzero degree of the spherical summand | ≥ | $2dm\\|\lambda_0\\|^2+b$ | divisible integers m |
| q-final-contradiction | scaled gap in the final contradiction | ≤ | $C-b$ | arbitrarily large m |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **s-cat-specialization** [claim](#stmt-cat-specialization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cat-specialization): Relative cell stratification over a henselian trait with fibrewise comparison -> enhanced equivalence of parahoric categories in all characteristics
- **s-cat-satake** [claim](#stmt-cat-satake) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cat-satake): Purity of intersection complexes and weight truncation formality -> derived Satake with Frobenius dilation
- **s-cat-central** [claim](#stmt-cat-central) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cat-central): Nearby-cycles central sheaves and graded interchange -> central functor with Wakimoto filtration
- **s-cat-springer** [claim](#stmt-cat-springer) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cat-springer): Section algebra calculation and adjunction with Yoneda -> Springer test identification
- **s-cat-cyclic** [claim](#stmt-cat-cyclic) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cat-cyclic): Path stacks and balanced bar construction in the enhanced formalism -> coherent path rotation functor
- **s-cat-hecke** [claim](#stmt-cat-hecke) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cat-hecke): Rigid kernel characters and closed-kernel computation -> Hecke action and transfers
- **s-cat-loops** [claim](#stmt-cat-loops) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cat-loops): Relative tensor product of module categories and representing object -> module pairing over the loop algebra
- **s-amp-geometry** [claim](#stmt-amp-geometry) / [proof](#proof-stmt-amp-geometry): Canonical reductions, bounded families and slope estimates -> bounded geometry of bundle stacks
- **s-amp-trace-bound** [claim](#stmt-amp-trace-bound) / [proof](#proof-stmt-amp-trace-bound): Stratification by stabilizer dimension and perverse support criterion -> uniform perverse bound
- **s-amp-tests** [claim](#stmt-amp-tests) / [proof](#proof-stmt-amp-tests): Costandard Hecke strata with affine vanishing -> uniform geometric tests
- **s-amp-specialization** [claim](#stmt-amp-specialization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-amp-specialization): Etale slice and elimination of contractible Koszul pairs -> specialized equivariant dg module
- **s-amp-levi** [claim](#stmt-amp-levi) / [proof](#proof-stmt-amp-levi): Opposite partial flags and localization by nef sections -> uniform central-character tests
- **s-amp-orbits** [claim](#stmt-amp-orbits) / [proof](#proof-stmt-amp-orbits): Jacobson-Morozov triples fixed by a diagonalizable action -> finite orbit structure with nonpositive normal weights
- **s-amp-support** [claim](#stmt-amp-support) / [proof](#proof-stmt-amp-support): Local cohomology along an orbit and exterior cochains -> orbit contribution bound
- **s-amp-fiber** [claim](#stmt-amp-fiber) / [proof](#proof-stmt-amp-fiber): Induction on semisimple rank with open and boundary orbits -> bounded corrected fibers
- **s-amp-amplitude** [claim](#stmt-amp-amplitude) / [proof](#proof-stmt-amp-amplitude): Unique extreme weight line and squared-norm identity -> extreme amplitude bound with linear gap
- **s-rot-bounded** [claim](#stmt-rot-bounded) / [proof](#proof-stmt-rot-bounded): Preserved parabolic reduction contradicting genericity, inertia and valuative criterion -> finite type Deligne-Mumford shtuka stack
- **s-rot-finite-correspondence** [claim](#stmt-rot-finite-correspondence) / [proof](#proof-stmt-rot-finite-correspondence): Gallery steps and rank-one panel computation -> translation as finite etale correspondence
- **s-rot-concatenation** [claim](#stmt-rot-concatenation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-rot-concatenation): Concatenation of path steps and large-Frobenius trace formula -> trace as shifted loop count
- **s-rot-anisotropic** [claim](#stmt-rot-anisotropic) / [proof](#proof-stmt-rot-anisotropic): Product formula applied to a fundamental character -> no noncentral split directions
- **s-rot-picard** [claim](#stmt-rot-picard) / [proof](#proof-stmt-rot-picard): Connected generalized Picard group, norm lift and Lang's theorem -> equal ordinary and shifted counts
- **s-rot-trace** [claim](#stmt-rot-trace) / [proof](#proof-stmt-rot-picard-2): Assembling loop counts and normalizations -> rotation trace identity
- **s-ext-hecke** [claim](#stmt-ext-hecke) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ext-hecke): Spherical cyclicity of principal series and Clifford descent -> weight ideal equals hyperspecial ideal
- **s-ext-hecke-finite** [claim](#stmt-ext-hecke-finite) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ext-hecke-finite): Finite expression of idempotents in the ideal -> transfer of finiteness to hyperspecial parts
- **s-ext-spectral** [claim](#stmt-ext-spectral) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ext-spectral): Spectral theorem at fixed level and constant term decay -> finite-level spectral facts
- **s-ext-matrix-bound** [claim](#stmt-ext-matrix-bound) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ext-matrix-bound): Resolvent bound on a circle and contour formula -> uniform power bound
- **s-ext-spectral-gap** [claim](#stmt-ext-spectral-gap) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ext-spectral-gap): Annihilating polynomial and strict orthogonal projection inequality -> spectral gap filter
- **s-ext-lower** [claim](#stmt-ext-lower) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ext-lower): Trace comparison and compact support weight bound -> nonvanishing in a high degree
- **s-ext-rationality** [claim](#stmt-ext-rationality) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ext-rationality): Difference of integrality conditions over a cone -> rational exponent
- **s-ext-unique-extreme** [claim](#stmt-ext-unique-extreme) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ext-unique-extreme): Weights as highest weight minus positive roots -> unique maximizing weight
- **s-ext-extreme-transfer** [claim](#stmt-ext-extreme-transfer) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ext-extreme-transfer): Wakimoto spectral sequence and Hensel factorization of an annihilator -> extreme part transferred to spherical level
- **s-ext-spherical-projector** [claim](#stmt-ext-spherical-projector) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ext-spherical-projector): Cayley-Hamilton on the slide matrix and Bezout idempotent -> dg direct summand
- **s-ext-koszul** [claim](#stmt-ext-koszul) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ext-koszul): Koszul spectral sequence and Nakayama's lemma -> top cohomology survives specialization
- **s-ext-discrete** [claim](#stmt-ext-discrete) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ext-koszul-2): Induction on semisimple rank with a contradiction between lower and upper degree bounds -> compact times Jacobson-Morozov form of dominant weights
- **s-glob-hecke-spectrum** [claim](#stmt-glob-hecke-spectrum) / [proof](#proof-stmt-glob-hecke-spectrum): Commuting normal operators on a finite-dimensional space -> joint eigenspace decomposition
- **s-glob-embeddings** [claim](#stmt-glob-embeddings) / [proof](#proof-stmt-glob-embeddings): Torus alignment, invariant form argument and open orbit uniqueness -> unique orbit and algebraic triple
- **s-glob-signs** [claim](#stmt-glob-signs) / [proof](#stmt-glob-signs): Square root sign change gives a finite central element -> disjoint Galois-stable support sets
- **s-glob-twist-purity** [claim](#stmt-glob-twist-purity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-glob-twist-purity): Determinant normalization and purity of irreducible lisse sheaves -> place-independent logarithmic absolute values
- **s-glob-place** [claim](#stmt-glob-place) / [proof](#proof-stmt-glob-place): Global parameter evaluated on highest weight representations -> place-independent dominant exponent
- **s-glob-descent** [claim](#stmt-glob-descent) / [proof](#proof-stmt-glob-descent): Galois-fixed idempotents partitioning the joint spectrum by orbit -> rational orbit decomposition
- **s-main-decomposition** [claim](#stmt-main-decomposition) / [proof](#proof-stmt-glob-descent-2): Scalar extension of the rational decomposition -> rational and l-adic decompositions
- **s-main-generic-ramanujan** [claim](#stmt-main-generic-ramanujan) / [proof](#proof-stmt-glob-descent-3): Common orbit plus local geometric-parameter criterion forces the trivial orbit -> temperedness at all unramified places

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-orbit-decomposition**: At every full finite level with arbitrary divisor multiplicities, the cuspidal function space is the direct sum, over nilpotent orbits of the dual group, of the spectral-support summands. This holds for both the rational and the l-adic coefficients.
- **inv-unramified-temperedness**: For a split connected adjoint absolutely simple group, a cuspidal automorphic representation with one unramified generic place is tempered at every place where it is unramified.
- **inv-local-dominant-weight**: A dominant ordered Bernstein weight of a unitary discrete automorphic representation at Iwahori level has the compact-times-Jacobson-Morozov form.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-number-fields**: Apply the orbit decomposition to automorphic forms over a number field or any field with archimedean places. -> The proofs use shtukas, Frobenius and geometric Satake over a curve over a finite field, none of which exist over a number field.
- **anti-ramified-places**: Conclude temperedness of ramified local components from the corollary. -> The conclusion covers only the places at which the representation is unramified, so nothing is proved at ramified places.
- **anti-non-adjoint**: Apply the generic-implies-tempered corollary to a non-adjoint or non-simple split group. -> The proof uses that the dual group is simply connected and the local geometric-parameter criterion for adjoint groups; outside this scope the argument is not available.
- **anti-executable-algorithm**: Treat the decomposition as an executable procedure with a runtime bound. -> The result is a structural isomorphism proved by geometric and spectral arguments; no finite procedure or complexity bound is given.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The results are existence and isomorphism theorems for cuspidal spaces, obtained by derived Satake, shtuka cohomology, degree bounds and Lafforgue parameters. The main theorem states that the natural addition maps are isomorphisms and no finite procedure for computing the summands is given, so there is no constructive or algorithmic content to execute.

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-function-field-semisimple-setting** (hypothesis): The decomposition is proved only for a split connected semisimple group over a finite field, on a smooth projective geometrically connected curve, with global field its function field. Nothing is claimed for number fields, non-split groups or non-semisimple groups. Blocked use: ['Applying the orbit decomposition to automorphic forms over a number field', 'Applying it to a non-split or non-semisimple group without a new argument']
- **limit-cuspidal-space-only** (formal_scope): The decomposition concerns the space of cuspidal functions, defined by vanishing of unipotent integrals along every proper parabolic. It says nothing about the continuous spectrum, Eisenstein series or non-cuspidal functions. Blocked use: ['Using orbit summands for Eisenstein series or non-cuspidal automorphic forms', 'Reading the decomposition as a statement about the full space of automorphic functions']
- **limit-unramified-places-only** (edge_case): The temperedness corollary concludes only at places where the representation is unramified. Ramified local components are outside its conclusion. Blocked use: ['Concluding temperedness of ramified local components from the corollary', 'Using the corollary as a bound on ramified Satake-type parameters']
- **limit-adjoint-absolutely-simple-group** (hypothesis): The generic-implies-tempered corollary is stated only for a split connected adjoint absolutely simple group. Its proof uses that the dual group is simply connected together with a local geometric-parameter criterion, so other group types are not covered. Blocked use: ['Applying the corollary to a non-adjoint or non-simple split group', 'Applying the corollary to a non-split group']
- **limit-local-theorem-hypotheses** (hypothesis): The local compact-times-Jacobson-Morozov form is proved for unitary discrete automorphic representations at Iwahori level at the chosen closed point, and only for ordered Bernstein weights with dominant exponent. Non-dominant weights, non-unitary or non-discrete representations are not covered. Blocked use: ['Applying the local form to a non-dominant ordered Bernstein weight', 'Applying it to a non-unitary or continuous-spectrum representation']
- **limit-nonconstructive-local-proof** (nonconstructive): The local theorem is obtained by contradiction and induction on semisimple rank, comparing two degree bounds on one summand. No finite procedure that computes the orbit, the compact factor or the summands is given. Blocked use: ['Presenting the decomposition as an algorithm with a runtime bound', 'Extracting a computable orbit label for a given cusp form from this paper alone']
- **limit-large-iterate-threshold** (edge_case): The rotation trace identity is asserted only for sufficiently large iterates, and the paper states no explicit threshold. The identity should not be used at a specific small iterate. Blocked use: ['Using the trace identity at a particular small iterate without a threshold', 'Quoting an explicit range of validity for the identity']
- **limit-amplitude-constant-ineffective** (approximation): The amplitude estimate gives an upper bound with a constant independent of the scaling parameter but with no stated value. It supports an asymptotic contradiction, not a numerical bound. Blocked use: ['Using the amplitude bound as a numerical estimate for a concrete weight', 'Treating the constant as effective or computed']
- **limit-characteristic-comparison** (formal_scope): The comparison between characteristic zero and positive characteristic requires the residue characteristic to differ from the coefficient prime. The categorical results do not apply with coefficients of the same characteristic as the residue field. Blocked use: ['Transporting the enhanced equivalences to the residue characteristic equal to the coefficient prime', 'Using the specialization with modular coefficients']

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove rational and $`\overline{\mathbb Q}_\ell`$ decompositions of cuspidal automorphic functions for split semisimple groups over global function fields into subspaces indexed by nilpotent orbits of the dual group. The decompositions hold at every full finite level, with arbitrary divisor multiplicities. Each summand is governed by the same orbit at every unramified place and every complex embedding. At trivial level this proves Conjectures 3.4.5 and 3.4.6 of Gaitsgory–Lafforgue–Raskin. For split connected adjoint absolutely simple groups, we also prove that a cuspidal automorphic representation with one generic unramified local component is tempered at every unramified place. Thus globally generic cuspidal representations in this scope satisfy the unramified part of the generalized Ramanujan conjecture.

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

The absolute values of unramified Hecke eigenvalues measure the departure of an automorphic representation from temperedness. The Ramanujan–Arthur prediction organizes the departures that occur in the discrete spectrum by homomorphisms from $\mathrm{SL}_2$ into the Langlands dual group; the additional algebraic factor is central to Arthur’s conjectural description of non-tempered discrete automorphic representations (Arthur 1989, sec. 4 and 8) <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[Arthur1989](#ref-Arthur1989)]. For cuspidal automorphic functions over a global function field, this leads to a decomposition indexed by nilpotent orbits. We prove that decomposition with arbitrary full finite level. Its common orbit also turns genericity at one unramified place into temperedness at all unramified places in the adjoint absolutely simple case.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### The statement
